  - fix: Send a short stable code, not the message: keep a small map of expected cases (`expired`, `denied`, `not_configured`, `failed`) and redirect with `?error=failed&id=<correlation id>` for anything else, logging the detail server-side with `console.error` as lib/api.ts:26 already does. Line 8's pass-through of the provider's own `error` parameter should be allowlisted to the OAuth 2.0 error codes rather than url-encoded verbatim.
  - ruling: apps/web/app/api/integrations/[provider]/callback/route.ts:13 url-encodes e.message into a 302 Location, and the message for any database failure is drizzle's `Failed query: <sql>\nparams: <values>` (verified in drizzle-orm 0.45.2 errors.js:10-19). It is a second, unwrapped copy of SECRET-4's defect in one of the handlers that does not use route(), and the destination is worse than a response body - browser history, referrers and access logs.
- **P3** `SECRET-NEW6` Playwright keeps a trace of every failed e2e run, including the session cookie and the committed password, and the config takes its base URL from an environment variable that has been pointed at production - `trace: "retain-on-failure"` writes a trace.zip containing the run's cookies, request bodies and a screencast, and `baseURL` is whatever `E2E_BASE_URL` says. The brief records that e2e has been run against the production database, so the same configuration that produced the stray `@kettleworth.test` accounts also produces, on any failure, a local artefact holding a live production session token for one of them.
  - where: `apps/web/playwright.config.ts:1`
  - fails when: A developer repeats what the brief records as having already happened - runs the suite against the live site to check a release - and one assertion fails. Playwright writes a trace holding that run's Set-Cookie session token for a real production account, plus the request bodies and a screencast, to a local directory that nothing cleans up. No attacker path: the directory is gitignored and never leaves the machine unless someone shares it, which is what holds this at P3. No production token is on disk today.
  - fix: Refuse to run against a non-local base URL by default: in apps/web/playwright.config.ts throw unless `E2E_BASE_URL` is empty, localhost, or an explicit `E2E_ALLOW_REMOTE=1` is set, and set `trace: process.env.E2E_BASE_URL ? "off" : "retain-on-failure"` so a remote run cannot leave a token on disk. Delete the two existing trace.zip files and add a `pnpm` script that clears `apps/web/test-results` after a run. Pair this with SECRET-3's env-var password change so a production run has no committed credential to capture in the first place.
  - ruling: apps/web/playwright.config.ts:6 sets trace: "retain-on-failure" with baseURL taken from E2E_BASE_URL, and :8 drops the local web server whenever that variable is set, so pointing the suite at production is a one-variable change. I opened both trace.zip files in apps/web/test-results: they do contain kw.session_token and the committed password, so the artefact really does capture credentials. They are from a localhost:3100 run, not a production one, and test-results is gitignored.
- **P3** `TEST-9` A test that cannot fail: the sound-setting test asserts a return type, not the fallback it claims to protect - The test named for the invariant 'an unreadable cookie cannot silence the timer' asserts only that `audible()` returns a boolean - which the type system already guarantees for every input it passes - and the real fallback lives in a file with no test task.
  - where: `packages/core/src/sound.test.ts:84`
  - fails when: A fourth sound mode is added and audible is tightened to a condition that returns false for it, silencing the rest timer the comment says must never be silenced - and sound.test.ts:84 still passes, because false is a boolean. Equally, if the includes() check in sound-pref.ts is dropped, a cookie of kw-sound=garbage is returned as a mode and no test anywhere notices.
  - fix: Assert the value, not the type: `expect(audible(mode, "rest")).toBe(mode !== "off")` for each mode, and move the real assertion to where the fallback is - a test on `soundMode()` that a cookie of an unknown value yields "on" - once apps/web has a test task. In quests.test.ts:62, add `expect(a.moves.length).toBeGreaterThan(0)` so the rest of that test cannot pass vacuously.
  - ruling: I read the test. sound.test.ts:83-86 asserts `typeof audible(mode, "rest")` is "boolean" for each mode and that SOUND_MODES[0] === "on"; audible (sound.ts:68-72) returns a boolean on all three paths, so the loop cannot fail, and the second assertion pins an array order rather than a fallback. The behaviour the test's title claims to protect lives in apps/web/lib/sound-pref.ts, in a package with no test script. The secondary case holds too: in quests.test.ts:62-68 the toEqual, the not.toContain and both for loops all pass if moves is empty, and nothing in that test requires it not to be.
- **P3** `TEST-N5` The rest-timer regression tests ride a real wall clock and real sleeps, with one CI retry to paper over the result - The two assertions written for the owner's reported timer regressions are timing comparisons against the device clock after fixed `waitForTimeout` sleeps, inside a polling helper whose deadline expires silently, and playwright.config.ts enables one retry in CI. 09-tests names real clocks, real sleeps and retry-on-red as the flakiness pattern that trains a team to re-run red.
  - where: `apps/web/e2e/core-flow.spec.ts:161`
  - fails when: On a loaded runner the intake follow-up takes longer than the 40-second settle deadline. The loop exits silently, the next line clicks a Continue that is not there, and the test fails 150 seconds later with a locator timeout that says nothing about the cause. The single retry passes and CI is green. The same shape hides the genuine case: a frozen timer satisfies toBeLessThanOrEqual, so a real regression can pass on the first attempt rather than the second.
  - fix: Assert the invariant rather than the elapsed time: compare the timer against the deadline the player published (`--kw-dock`/`data-clock` already expose state) or drive the clock with `page.clock.install()`/`fastForward`, which Playwright 1.63 supports, so no sleep is needed. Make `settle()` throw with a named error on deadline expiry instead of returning, tighten line 172 to `toBeLessThan(beforeMove)`, and set `retries: 0` until the suite has been green in CI for a while - with TEST-1 unfixed, the retry has never masked anything yet, which is the moment to remove it.
  - ruling: I read the spec. The timer assertions are clock comparisons after fixed sleeps: line ~161 takes `started`, does `await page.waitForTimeout(2_500)` and asserts the clock is less than started; line ~172 asserts `toBeLessThanOrEqual(beforeMove)`, which also passes if the clock has not moved at all - i.e. if it has frozen, one of the two regressions the test was written for. The intake helper at :81-90 is a `while (Date.now() < deadline)` loop with 300-400 ms sleeps that falls out silently on expiry with no assertion and no message, and playwright.config.ts:5 enables one retry in CI.
- **P3** `TEST-N8` A test whose only assertion is on the array it just built, whose real effect is rewriting a tracked file in the repository - The seventh test in case-study.test.ts writes docs/CASE-STUDIES.md and then asserts only that the module-level array it accumulated has more than 20 entries - a value the test itself constructed, which 09-tests lists under tests that cannot fail. It never reads the file back, so a wrong path, a truncated write or a permission failure is only caught by writeFileSync throwing. It also means `pnpm test` mutates a committed file, and the write depends on the six tests above it having run first.
  - where: `packages/core/src/programme/case-study.test.ts:75`
  - fails when: The engine's rationale strings change. A developer runs `pnpm test`, it passes, and `git status` shows docs/CASE-STUDIES.md modified for reasons unrelated to their work - so the natural moves are to check it out, discarding the regenerated record the README points at, or to commit it blindly inside an unrelated change. The reverse is worse: because mkdirSync creates whatever the relative path resolves to, moving the test file one level deeper writes the document into a new folder, the committed file silently stops being refreshed, and the test still passes, because its only assertion cannot notice.
  - fix: Make the document an output, not a test: move the generation into a script (`pnpm --filter @kettleworth/core docs:case-studies`) and have the test assert the committed file matches what the engine produces now - read docs/CASE-STUDIES.md and compare, so drift is a red test rather than a silent rewrite. If it stays a test, assert the file: `expect(readFileSync(out, "utf8")).toContain("## " + personas[0].name)` for each persona, which also removes the dependence on the six earlier tests having populated `lines`.
  - ruling: case-study.test.ts accumulates a module-scope `lines` array across the six persona tests and then, at :75-80, writes docs/CASE-STUDIES.md with writeFileSync and asserts only `expect(lines.length).toBeGreaterThan(20)` - a value the test itself built, and it never reads the file back. The file is tracked in git, so `pnpm test` mutates a committed file, and the write depends on the six tests above it having run first.

## Unproven

Neither side proved these, and the arbiter could not either. Each says what evidence would
settle it. These are questions for the owner, not maybes to bury.

- **P2** `SEC-NEW3` The dependency bump SEC-7 prescribes (next >= 16.4.1) leaves four further Next advisories open, and one of them re-opens the SVG path that is the only thing currently standing between the live image optimizer and unauthenticated RCE - `pnpm audit` reports eleven Next.js advisories against the installed 16.3.5, not the two SEC-7 names. Four of them are not fixed until >= 16.4.5, so the prescribed ">=16.4.1" would be verified as a fix and still leave them open. The important one is GHSA-7qx7-33qx-rqx2, an SVG rejection bypass in the image optimizer: the default SVG rejection is the only reason GHSA-2464-fhr4-w9xw (critical, unauthenticated RCE through the image optimizer when SVG files are used, with sharp 0.35.4 installed) is not already live on this deployment, because next.config.ts does not set dangerouslyAllowSVG. GHSA-f3mh-rxg3-f5g2, also >= 16.4.5, exposes HttpOnly cookies through an internal response header, which bears directly on the session contract the brief says must not change.
  - where: `apps/web/package.json:32`
  - fails when: An attacker pushes a crafted SVG to any public GitHub repository, so it is served from raw.githubusercontent.com - an allowlisted host in next.config.ts:10 - and requests it unauthenticated through `https://kettleworth.vercel.app/_next/image?url=...&w=640&q=75`. On 16.3.5 the SVG rejection that answers 400 today is bypassable (GHSA-7qx7-33qx-rqx2), so the bytes reach the image pipeline and librsvg through sharp 0.35.4, which is the RCE condition GHSA-2464-fhr4-w9xw describes on glibc Linux - the Vercel runtime. The team applies SEC-7's fix, bumps to 16.4.1, re-runs the audit, sees GHSA-2464 cleared and ships; GHSA-7qx7-33qx-rqx2 and GHSA-f3mh-rxg3-f5g2 are still open, and the second of those leaks the session cookie through an internal response header.
  - fix: Bump next to >= 16.4.5 (not 16.4.1) and let the package manager pull sharp >= 0.35.5, then re-run `pnpm audit` and confirm zero runtime-reachable Next or sharp advisories remain. Belt and braces while the bump is in flight: the image optimizer is not used by any component in this app (no next/image import anywhere in apps/web, components or packages/ui), so `images.remotePatterns` can be emptied, which per GHSA-cjq9-62q9-8jv4 takes the app out of scope for the SSRF entirely and shuts the optimizer surface.
  - ruling: I can verify the inputs but not the claim. next is installed at 16.3.5 and the image optimizer is live and unauthenticated (I fetched it: an allowlisted-host PNG returns 200 image/png, a non-allowlisted host returns 400). What I cannot establish from this checkout is the advisory set for 16.3.5, each advisory's first patched version, or whether the SVG rejection that currently answers 400 is bypassable on this version - and I would not take a dependency-upgrade floor on assertion when the whole point of the finding is that the floor is wrong.

## Never ruled on

A finding here was raised and no arbiter decided it, so its dimension reads UNKNOWN. The
review did not finish.

_none - every finding was ruled on_

## Rejected

Raised by an auditor and killed by the panel. Recorded so the next run does not re-raise them.

- `AI-3` /api/cron/weekly fails open: with CRON_SECRET unset or blank the whole AI-and-email fan-out is an unauthenticated GET - Same defect at the same line as SEC-1 (apps/web/app/api/cron/weekly/route.ts:9). Confirmed there, at P2 rather than P0: I listed the production environment myself (vercel env ls production, project basil-s-projects11/kettleworth) and CRON_SECRET is one of its nine variables, so the guard is active on what ships. This finding's evidence sentence - that apps/web/.env mirrors the production variable set - is not true; .env is a localhost file.
- `AI-8` Prompts and model outputs containing health data are written to ai_log in plaintext, with no retention and no link to the user row, so they survive the GDPR hard delete - Same defect at the same line as SECRET-1 (packages/api/src/ai/client.ts:62). Confirmed there at P1, including the retention and hard-delete halves of this claim.
- `AI-NEW5` AI-5's fix covers four profile fields; five more uncapped fields reach the same prompts, so the amplification survives the fix - The additional fields are real - lovedExerciseIds (packages/types/src/profile.ts:119), hatedExerciseIds (:120), knownLifts (:152) and rituals.timezone (:134) are unbounded, and tasks.ts:20 and :44 serialise the whole profile object rather than a field list - but this is the same defect and the same fix site as AI-5, which I confirmed with needs_debate set precisely because its fix scope is too narrow. Recorded there rather than twice.
- `CODE-14` The AI client's timeout and retry budget (up to 360 s) exceeds any route duration declared in the app, and the spend log is written only after the call returns - Duplicate of PERF-8 - the same `new Anthropic({ timeout: 120_000, maxRetries: 2 })` at ai/client.ts:14 and the same post-call ai_log write at client.ts:61-62. Confirmed there at P1, including the missing maxDuration on every AI route but /api/nutrition/estimate.
- `CODE-3` The weekly letter cron is one sequential loop over all users in a single request, with per-user failures swallowed and no user id recorded - Duplicate of PERF-1, which is the same serial loop at the same line and is confirmed there at P0. The additional observation here - that `.catch(() => null)` on runWeeklyAdaptation and `catch { failed++ }` record neither the user nor the reason - is folded into that ruling.
- `CODE-NEW6` The offline warm-up leaks a service-worker message listener on every refresh of Today, so one background-sync wakeup starts many concurrent queue flushes - Duplicate of PERF-12 - the same unremoved service-worker message listener at offline-warmup.tsx:24 against the cleanup at 51-55, and the same non-re-entrant flush(). Confirmed there at P2, including the `images` dependency that re-runs the effect on every RSC refresh.
- `CODE-NEW9` Newborn mode's week grouping buckets by calendar month plus epoch-week, which lands on a Thursday, so a three-day plan never loses a session - Duplicate of LEAN-NEW1, which covers the same epoch-week bucket at life-mode.ts:48 and also the second copy at adaptation.ts:23. Confirmed there at P1, with the key arithmetic re-derived independently.
- `LEAN-1` One 409 contract, two readings 14 lines apart: the offline flush deletes a queued set the server rejected and reports it as sent - Duplicate of CODE-1 - the same line, offline-queue.ts:22, and the same defect. Confirmed there at P1, including the two-readings-of-one-contract framing and the absent attempt cap.
- `LEAN-7` The weekly-letter cron's WHERE clause compares a NOT NULL column to itself: a filter that filters nothing, contradicting its own comment - Duplicate of CODE-2 - the same tautological predicate at apps/web/app/api/cron/weekly/route.ts:10. Confirmed there at P1, which is the severity this challenge argued for.
- `LEAN-NEW9` Four copies of a hand-rolled SQL id-list, built by string concatenation, in two services that already import drizzle's inArray - Duplicate of CODE-NEW10 - the same four sql.raw id lists at coach.ts:24/:28 and adaptation.ts:52/:58. Confirmed there at P3, with the same conclusion that no attacker-controlled value reaches them today.
- `OPS-8` The weekly letter cron is a serial unbounded loop with no maxDuration, no cursor, no retry and no dead letter, and its failures are swallowed into an integer nobody reads - Duplicate of SCALE-1. Same file and line (apps/web/app/api/cron/weekly/route.ts:12), same defect (one serial unbounded loop over every profile, one AI call and one email per iteration, inside one invocation, with no cursor, batch, lease, retry or dead letter) and the same fix. SCALE-1 is confirmed at P0; the missing maxDuration and the swallowed per-user `catch { failed++ }` are part of that finding's record, not a second finding.
- `OPS-9` The e2e suite creates real accounts and writes health data to whatever host E2E_BASE_URL names, with no guard against production - Duplicate of TEST-6. Same file and line (apps/web/playwright.config.ts:6), same defect (E2E_BASE_URL retargets a write-heavy suite at any host with no check on what that host is) and the same fix. TEST-6 is confirmed at P1 and additionally covers the missing teardown, so it is the fuller statement of the same defect.
- `PERF-10` The cron's "onboarded users" filter is a tautology, so it reads every profile row ever created and emails users who never finished intake - Duplicate of CODE-2 - the same tautological predicate at apps/web/app/api/cron/weekly/route.ts:10, confirmed there at P1. The unbounded full-table select it causes is part of PERF-1's confirmed scenario.
- `PERF-9` The GDPR export runs one query per training session in a serial loop, after a throwaway query for the first session - Duplicate of CODE-10 - the same account.ts:23 discarded select followed by one query per session id. Confirmed there at P2, including the serialisation of the whole document in memory.
- `PERF-NEW6` The challenge leaderboard fans every participant id into an IN list with no cap, which fails outright past 65,534 parameters - latent only because the challenge seeder can never run twice - Duplicate of CODE-NEW2 - the same unbounded entrant fetch and uncapped inArray lists in listChallenges at community.ts:205. Confirmed there at P1; the measured 65,534-parameter ceiling and the observation that ensureDefaultChallenges can never reseed over an existing row are folded into that ruling. The 'latent' argument rests on the dev database's expired challenge rows, which says nothing about production: on any database with no challenge row, listChallenges itself seeds two live ones on its first call.
- `PERF-NEW7` findMatches selects every non-private community profile with its whole TrainingProfile jsonb and ranks them in memory - one environment variable away from a page that cannot render - Duplicate of CODE-NEW5 - the same unbounded findMatches query at community.ts:94 behind the same COMMUNITY_DISCOVERY flag. Confirmed there at P3, with the flag as the reason the severity drops and with the instruction that it must not be flipped before the candidate set is bounded and indexed.
- `SCALE-NEW5` GDPR hard delete fails outright for any account that created a group or filed a report: two foreign keys are ON DELETE NO ACTION - Duplicate of OPS-NEW1. Same defect (group.created_by and report.reporter_id are ON DELETE NO ACTION, so account.ts:34's bare `delete(user)` raises a foreign-key violation), same evidence and same fix. OPS-NEW1 is confirmed at P1 and additionally verified the constraint set against the live schema, so it is the record to carry.
- `SCALE-NEW6` ai_log and audit_log have no foreign key to user, so free-text injury and medical data survives account deletion forever in two tables with no retention - Duplicate of OPS-NEW2. Same defect (ai_log.user_id and audit_log.user_id are bare text columns with no reference to user, so the rows survive the delete cascade, and ai_log holds the prompt - including injuries and medicalFlags - in plaintext with no retention), same lines and same fix. OPS-NEW2 is confirmed at P1 and also covers the absence of those tables from the GDPR export.
- `SEC-13` A tracked script runs against the production deployment and creates real accounts with a password committed to the repository - Same artefact and same defect as SECRET-3 (apps/web/live-multi.mjs, the hardcoded production base URL and the committed password). Confirmed there at P2.
- `SEC-14` Provider error text is returned verbatim to the caller, disclosing upstream endpoints and token-endpoint response bodies - Same line and same defect as SECRET-4 (apps/web/lib/api.ts:27 returning e.message to the caller). Confirmed there, with the corrected mechanism: the message that leaks is drizzle's DrizzleQueryError text, which carries the SQL and its bound parameters, not the provider URL this finding traced - and that path dies earlier anyway, because syncProvider throws 'Not connected' before any upstream call while no provider is configured.
- `SEC-NEW1` RESEND_API_KEY is unset in production, so every magic-link sign-in URL and every weekly letter is written in plaintext to the platform's runtime log instead of being sent, and magic-link sign-in silently does nothing - Same line and same defect as SECRET-2 (packages/api/src/email.ts:7, the console.log fallback). Confirmed there at P1, including the production fact this finding adds and that I checked myself: RESEND_API_KEY is not among the nine variables in the production environment, so the fallback is the live path.
- `SEC-NEW4` group.createdBy is a second ON DELETE NO ACTION foreign key to user, so the GDPR hard delete also fails for anyone who has created a group - and SEC-4's fix, which only handles report, leaves it - The second foreign key is real - packages/db/src/schema/community.ts:27 group.createdBy references user.id with no onDelete, and POST /api/community/groups (route.ts:5) makes createGroup reachable for any authenticated account - but it is the same defect with the same fix site as SEC-4, which I confirmed with needs_debate set for exactly this reason and named group.createdBy in the ruling. Recorded there rather than twice.
- `SEC-NEW7` POST /api/coach/letters/generate lets the client set force:true and email:true, defeating the only server-side idempotency guard on the weekly letter and turning the cron's per-user work into an unlimited self-service endpoint - Same line and same defect as AI-6 (apps/web/app/api/coach/letters/generate/route.ts:4 forwarding client force and email into generateWeeklyLetter). Confirmed there at P1.
- `SECRET-NEW1` The intake transcript - the user's own words about injuries and medical conditions - is stored in plaintext in profile.intake_transcript, and the AI summary derived from it in profile.ai_summary, both outside the encryption the privacy policy promises and both missing from the GDPR export - Same defect and same fix as SEC-NEW5 (the plaintext profile.intake_transcript and profile.ai_summary beside the encrypted profile.sensitive), confirmed there at P2. One correction worth carrying over: the claim that neither column is in the GDPR export is half wrong - packages/api/src/services/account.ts:25 does return aiSummary in the export; intake_transcript is the one it omits.
- `SECRET-NEW2` The service worker stores progress photos, every /app page and every GET /api response in the device's Cache Storage and nothing clears it on sign-out or account deletion - Same line and same defect as SEC-12 (apps/web/app/sw.ts:24 and the defaultCache spread at :25), confirmed there and raised to P1 on the strength of the cached surface this finding also describes.
- `SECRET-NEW4` Two endpoint guards authenticate only if their secret happens to be set, and the Mux one is unset in every environment the repository describes, so the webhook signature check is dead code - The pattern is real and worth stating once, but both instances are already findings: the Mux guard is SEC-9 (confirmed at P2, including that MUX_WEBHOOK_SECRET is absent from the production environment, so the signature check is dead code) and the cron guard is SEC-1 (confirmed at P2). Nothing here is a third defect.
- `SECRET-NEW5` audit_log orphans the deleted user's id the same way ai_log does, and abandoned OAuth verification rows keep a user id with no cleanup - neither is covered by the SECRET-1 fix - audit_log does have the identical shape - packages/db/src/schema/meta.ts:22 declares user_id with no reference, and account.ts:33 writes a new row on the way past without deleting the user's existing ones - but that is the same defect and the same fix site as SECRET-1, which I confirmed with needs_debate set and named audit_log in the ruling. The verification half is unreachable: beginConnect cannot run in production because no provider is configured, so no abandoned oauth_* rows exist to orphan.
- `TEST-1` CI has never run a single test: 40 of 40 runs since 14 Sep die at pnpm setup before the suite starts - Duplicate of OPS-1. Same file and line (.github/workflows/ci.yml:21), same mechanism (pnpm/action-setup@v4 refuses a version input alongside package.json's packageManager, so all 40 runs die before install) and the same one-line fix. OPS-1 is confirmed at P1 and its record explicitly covers that no test, typecheck or e2e has ever executed in CI, which is this finding's claim.
- `TEST-N3` Seven route handlers never touch the shared gate, two of them fail open when a secret is unset, and no test covers any of them - Duplicate, in both of its halves. The fail-open gates it names - cron/weekly/route.ts:9 `if (secret && ...)` and webhooks/mux/route.ts:10 `if (secret) {` - are the evidence of OPS-2, confirmed at P1 with the same fix (validate configuration at boot and require the secret rather than skipping the check). The "no test asserts a refusal on any of the seven" half is TEST-4, confirmed at P1, which covers the absence of any negative auth test in any tier. The route-inventory observation about the seven bypassing handlers is OPS-NEW6. Nothing here is a defect that is not already in the record; I checked the code and did not verify whether CRON_SECRET is actually unset in production, and I deliberately did not probe /api/cron/weekly because doing so would generate letters and emails for real users.

## Contested questions and how they were decided

_none_

## Controls that already exist and work

- (ai-safety) {'control': 'Authentication before the model call: the shared route wrapper returns 401 when there is no better-auth session, before the body is even parsed', 'covers': 'all seven user-facing AI entry points (coach chat, letters generate, onboarding followup and complete, programme generate, nutrition mealplan and estimate, photos analyse); it does NOT cover /api/cron/weekly, which does not use the wrapper', 'file': 'apps/web/lib/api.ts', 'line': 10}
- (ai-safety) {'control': 'Zod body validation on every AI route, with a real length cap on the chat message', 'covers': 'the single coach-chat turn (`message: z.string().min(1).max(1000)`); it does not bound the 12-message history or the context JSON that are sent with it, and the equivalent caps are missing on /api/onboarding/followup (AI-2)', 'file': 'apps/web/app/api/coach/chat/route.ts', 'line': 5}
- (ai-safety) {'control': 'Structured output: every task returns a Zod-validated object with per-field length and range caps, not free prose, and `max_tokens` is set per task (400 to 1,500)', 'covers': 'the output bound on all 10 tasks - `tasks.ts:42,57,68,82,108-119` and `coach.ts:43,85` - so no single response can be arbitrarily large; the input side is what is unbounded', 'file': 'packages/api/src/ai/client.ts', 'line': 45}
- (ai-safety) {'control': 'Post-parse validators reject output the product cannot trust, and the rejection is logged and returns null', 'covers': "programme notes that name exercises outside the plan (tasks.ts:61), focus areas outside the muscle taxonomy (tasks.ts:100), and chat actions naming a session id that is not in the caller's own upcoming list (coach.ts:103)", 'file': 'packages/api/src/ai/tasks.ts', 'line': 61}
- (ai-safety) {'control': 'No agent loop and no tools: one `messages.parse` call per request, no tool definitions anywhere in the repository', 'covers': 'the whole step-cap and recursion class of runaway cost - there is no multi-step agent, no shell, HTTP, filesystem or raw-SQL tool exposed to the model (inventory `agent_tools: 0`, confirmed by grep)', 'file': 'packages/api/src/ai/client.ts', 'line': 43}
- (ai-safety) {'control': 'Deterministic fallback on every AI path: `structured()` returns null on refusal, parse failure, validation failure or any exception, and each caller has non-AI copy', 'covers': 'availability when AI is capped, refused or down - `tasks.ts:45,64,71`, `coach.ts:67,107`, `profile.ts` summary; this is what makes a spend cap implementable without breaking the product, but it does not refund a call that was already billed', 'file': 'packages/api/src/ai/client.ts', 'line': 67}
- (ai-safety) {'control': "Model-chosen actions execute under the requesting user's own id, scoped in SQL, and are re-validated by Zod before they reach the database", 'covers': 'cross-tenant writes from a bad prompt: `eq(trainingSession.userId, userId)` here, `eq(trainingSession.userId, userId)` in `session.ts:251`, and the action union at `coach.ts:78-85` plus `TrainingProfile.parse` in `upsertProfile` bound the payload shape', 'file': 'packages/api/src/services/coach.ts', 'line': 112}
- (ai-safety) {'control': 'Model output is rendered as React text, never as HTML', 'covers': 'XSS from an agent-produced string: `grep -rn dangerouslySetInnerHTML apps/web packages` returns no hits anywhere in the repository, so the chat reply, letters and photo summaries cannot carry markup', 'file': 'apps/web/components/coach/coach-view.tsx', 'line': 1}
- (ai-safety) {'control': 'Per-call provenance: account id, task, model, prompt, output, input and output token counts, latency and validity are recorded for every call', 'covers': 'the raw material a per-account cap and a cost report would read; nothing currently reads it, there is no index on `user_id`, and failed or retried attempts are not recorded (AI-9)', 'file': 'packages/api/src/ai/client.ts', 'line': 62}
- (ai-safety) {'control': 'Upload bound before the model sees an image: content-type allowlist and an 8 MB per-file cap on stored photos, media-type enum and a byte cap on the inline estimate image', 'covers': '`MAX_BYTES = 8 * 1024 * 1024` and `TYPES` at photos.ts:14-19 for stored progress photos, and `data: z.string().min(100).max(8_000_000)` plus `mediaType: z.enum([...])` at `apps/web/app/api/nutrition/estimate/route.ts:7`; the number of analyse calls per photo set is not bounded', 'file': 'packages/api/src/services/photos.ts', 'line': 14}
- (ai-safety) {'control': 'Week-keyed idempotency on the two most expensive per-user tasks, so an ordinary repeat request does not re-call the model', 'covers': 'the weekly letter per (userId, weekStartsOn) here, the meal plan per week at `nutrition.ts:39-42`, and the weekly adaptation via `adaptedAt` at `adaptation.ts:43`; all three are defeated by a client-supplied `force` flag (AI-6)', 'file': 'packages/api/src/services/coach.ts', 'line': 48}
- (ai-safety) {'control': "better-auth's own rate limiter, enabled with a 30-per-60-second window", 'covers': "only better-auth's own `/api/auth/*` paths (sign-in, sign-up, magic link), keyed on client IP plus path, with counters held in process memory because no `secondaryStorage` or `storage` is configured - verified in better-auth 1.7.4 at `dist/api/rate-limiter/index.mjs:235-266` and `dist/context/create-context.mjs:175`. It does not touch any AI route and it resets on every cold start", 'file': 'packages/api/src/auth.ts', 'line': 24}
- (ai-safety) {'control': 'An entitlement seam exists and already reads the `subscription` table, ready to gate AI per tier', 'covers': 'nothing today - `grep -rn entitlements` finds no caller outside the barrel export at `packages/api/src/index.ts:15`, and `BILLING_ENFORCED` is unset so `premium` is true for everyone; recorded because it is the hook the AI-1 fix plugs into, not as a control that currently bites', 'file': 'packages/api/src/services/entitlements.ts', 'line': 6}
- (altitude) {'control': 'One route-handler helper doing auth, zod parsing, uniform error shape and server logging, which is why 55 of 62 handlers are 3-4 lines with no try/catch/validate/respond boilerplate', 'covers': 'every handler that imports `route` from @/lib/api - all of app/api/** except photos/*, library, cron/weekly, webhooks/mux, auth/[...all] and integrations/[provider]/callback', 'file': 'apps/web/lib/api.ts', 'line': 7}
- (altitude) {'control': 'One structured-LLM call site: model, max_tokens, prompt caching, zod output parsing, post-parse validation, ai_log persistence and the null-on-failure fallback are written once', 'covers': 'all 7 AI tasks in packages/api/src/ai/tasks.ts (intake follow-up, extraction, profile summary, programme note, meal-plan note, body-photo read, meal estimate) plus coachChat and the weekly letter - no task re-implements the SDK call', 'file': 'packages/api/src/ai/client.ts', 'line': 36}
- (altitude) {'control': "Session lookup wrapped in React `cache`, so the per-page and per-layout `requireUser()` calls cost one auth round trip per request rather than fifteen - the repeated check is correct in Next.js (a layout's guard does not protect its pages) and is not redundant padding", 'covers': 'apps/web/app/app/layout.tsx:19 plus all 14 `/app/*` page components and every `route()` call', 'file': 'apps/web/lib/session.ts', 'line': 7}

- (altitude) {'control': 'No over-structure anywhere in the half of this dimension that usually fails: zero classes, zero abstract classes, zero `implements`, no DI container, no event bus, no repository layer over Drizzle, and exactly two exported interfaces in the whole repo (one of them the ten-implementation provider adapter above)', 'covers': "verified by `grep -rn 'EventEmitter|class .*Repository|class .*Factory|createContainer|abstract class|implements |^export class' packages apps/web --include=*.ts --include=*.tsx` excluding node_modules and .next - only matches are two mock classes in apps/web/e2e/core-flow.spec.ts:25-26", 'file': 'packages/integrations/src/adapter.ts', 'line': 17}
- (altitude) {'control': "No long functions: the inventory's `long_functions` list is empty at a 120-code-line threshold, and the largest service file holds 24 named exported functions in 442 lines rather than one 400-line procedure", 'covers': 'all of packages/api/src/services and packages/core/src; the 701-line apps/web/components/session/player.tsx is 10 separate components (SessionPlayer, PrescriptionNumerals, SetConsole, SwapList, Scale, FinishForm, Benchmark and three helpers), not one function', 'file': 'packages/api/src/services/session.ts', 'line': 10}

## What could not be assessed

- (ai-safety) Whether an Anthropic organisation-level spend cap or budget alert exists in the provider console. Nothing in the repository references one, and the provider console is not reachable from here, so the absolute ceiling on the bill cannot be stated as a number. If none exists, AI-1's worst case is bounded only by the organisation's rate limits.
- (ai-safety) Whether `CRON_SECRET` is actually set (and non-blank) in the Vercel production environment. `.env*` is gitignored and the deployment is not readable from here, which is why AI-3 carries medium confidence on reachability; the fail-open code pattern itself is certain.
- (ai-safety) The real list price of `claude-opus-5`. Every cost figure in this report uses ARCHITECTURE.md's own stated assumption (GBP 12 per million input tokens, GBP 60 per million output, ARCHITECTURE.md:161-162) because the repository states no other figure and vendor pricing could not be verified from this environment.
- (ai-safety) Today's actual per-account consumption. The production `ai_log` could not be read (no database credentials in this environment), so no statement is made about what the owner's and the test accounts' current usage is - only about what the code permits.
- (ai-safety) No request was executed against a running server: there is no dev server, no `DATABASE_URL` and no `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` here, and running the AI paths would spend the owner's money. Every finding is traced statically from an HTTP entry point to the provider call; the `repro` fields are written to be run by the owner against a local dev server, not against production.
- (ai-safety) Vercel's effective per-function request-body and duration limits for this project (plan-dependent). AI-2 and AI-9 state the application-level absence of a bound and use the documented Vercel serverless body limit of 4.5 MB as the only remaining ceiling; the exact platform numbers for this account were not verifiable.
- (ai-safety) There is no test anywhere that asserts any AI bound - no test file exists under `packages/api` at all (`find . -name '*.test.ts'` returns 19 files, all in `packages/core` and `packages/integrations`). So none of the controls in `mitigations_seen` is pinned by a test, and a regression in any of them would be silent. The AI-safety reference calls for exactly these tests (the 101st request refused, an oversized prompt rejected before the provider client is called, with the provider mocked); their absence is recorded here rather than as a separate finding because test coverage belongs to another dimension.
- (altitude) I could not execute any of the repro commands against a running instance: there is no local .env with DATABASE_URL, Neon is the only database, and the brief forbids writes to the project, so every failure scenario here is traced from source rather than observed. The traces for LEAN-1, LEAN-3 and LEAN-7 are complete from entry point to sink; a fixer should still confirm each with the stated curl or query before and after.
- (altitude) apps/web/public/sw.js is git-ignored and generated (apps/web/.gitignore lists it), and it appears in the inventory as an entry point with queue and timer hits. I did not audit it for duplication of the lib/offline-queue.ts logic, so the `kw-flush` sync handler may hold a third copy of the replay policy in LEAN-1. The generated file is not in the repository to read.
- (altitude) The branch `ai-policy-and-cron-batching` named in the brief is not checked out in this worktree (HEAD is kw-weight, 003fe0b). If packages/api/src/ai/policy.ts on that branch duplicates the fallback or tiering logic already in packages/api/src/ai/client.ts, that duplication is outside what I can see.
- (altitude) Whether `exercise_instance` rows are enumerable in practice - which sets the real severity ceiling of LEAN-4 - depends on whether any endpoint or page leaks instance ids across accounts. I checked the five instance routes and found none, but I did not sweep all 62 handlers' response bodies for foreign ids; dimension 2 is better placed to settle it.
- (deployability) Whether Neon point-in-time history or branch retention is configured on the production project, and what its window is. Not visible in the repository (apps/web/.vercel is absent and no Neon project id is committed) and I have no credential to query Neon read-only. This is the single fact that decides whether OPS-7 is P1 or P0: with no history window and no dump held elsewhere, unreconstructable health data and photographs have no recovery path at all.
- (deployability) Whether the production Vercel project actually has CRON_SECRET, MUX_WEBHOOK_SECRET, ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, SENTRY_DSN and PHOTO_STORAGE set, and which Node major and function duration the project is configured for. Vercel project settings are not in the repository and I did not query the Vercel API. OPS-2 scores MUX_WEBHOOK_SECRET as unset because DEPLOY.md's env list omits it entirely, which is the strongest evidence available from the repo, not because I read the production environment.
- (deployability) Whether a Vercel rollback has ever been performed on this project. Not recorded anywhere in the repository; the deployment history would show it but I did not query it.
- (deployability) The real wall-clock duration of one /api/cron/weekly invocation in production. I could not measure it: invoking it writes coach_letter rows and sends email to real addresses, which is outside a read-only audit. OPS-8's timing argument is therefore built from the code (one Claude call plus one email per iteration, serial) and DEPLOY.md's own statement of the platform default, not from an observed run.
- (deployability) Whether a rolled-back deploy serves stale asset URLs to clients holding a version-N service worker precache. The service worker is only compiled in a production build (next.config.ts:4) and testing this needs two deployed versions and a real browser profile, which a read-only pass cannot do. Flagged rather than scored.
- (deployability) Whether apps/web lints clean under rules it does not currently have: only @kettleworth/web defines a lint script, so the six packages under packages/* are unlinted by any command, and I could not tell whether that is deliberate.
- (performance) No production access, so every number here is either measured against the local dev database (150 users, 5,053 training sessions, 30,246 exercise instances, 855 exercises) or derived from the brief's design point. Production row counts, Neon's actual compute size and max_connections, and the real round-trip latency from the lhr1 region to the Neon endpoint were not observable.
- (performance) The exact platform function-duration limit that applies to the Vercel deployment (plan and fluid-compute settings) could not be read from the repository; only /api/nutrition/estimate declares maxDuration. PERF-1 and PERF-8 therefore state that the limit is reached, not what it is.
- (performance) No end-to-end latency measurement of any route: no dev server was started and no request was made against production, so all per-request figures are query counts and payload sizes multiplied by an assumed round-trip time, stated as such.
- (performance) Real-world photo sizes are unknown - the dev database holds no progress_photo rows, so PERF-6's byte figures come from the 8 MB cap in code rather than from measured uploads.
- (performance) Client-side rendering cost of the 701-line session player and the three per-world composition sets was not profiled; a phone-profile pass belongs with the UI pipeline, and no UI file was changed here.
- (performance) The repository contains no load test of any kind (no k6, autocannon, artillery or benchmark script in any package.json), so there is no committed baseline to compare a fix against; PERF-1's verdict rests on arithmetic, not on a run.
- (quality) The deployed Vercel function duration limit and plan are not readable from the repo, so the exact point at which the cron (CODE-3) and the AI routes (CODE-14) are killed is unverified. What is verifiable from the repo is the mismatch: a 360 s client budget against a single 60 s declaration and no declaration anywhere else.
- (quality) No database or dev server was available in this run, so no finding was executed end to end. Everything here is from reading the code plus `pnpm run typecheck` (7/7 clean), `pnpm run lint` (0 errors, 11 @next/next/no-img-element warnings) and `pnpm run test` (131 passing, matching the recorded baseline). The e2e suite needs a running app and Postgres.
- (quality) apps/web/public/sw.js is generated by Serwist at build time and gitignored, so the shipped service worker was not audited; only its source, apps/web/app/sw.ts, was.
- (quality) Whether the deployment sets TZ to anything other than UTC. If it does, the direction of the date errors in CODE-5 changes but not their existence, because the code never consults the user's zone on those paths.
- (quality) packages/api, packages/db, packages/core and packages/integrations have no lint task (only @kettleworth/web ran under `turbo run lint`), so no linter has ever looked at the server code in this audit's scope. Whether that is a CI gap belongs to dimension 8.
- (quality) Deliberately left to their owners: per-account AI caps and rate limiting (dimension 3), the missing authorisation on addComment/reportContent targets and the unsigned webhook/cron when their secrets are unset (dimension 2), photos as bytea in Postgres, the module-level 10-connection pool in packages/db/src/index.ts:13 and the unbounded full-table reads in community.ts:94 and nutrition.ts:40 (dimension 7).
- (scalability) No access to the production or a staging environment and no database with representative data, so every row-count, query-plan and seq-scan claim above is derived from the schema, the code and ARCHITECTURE.md's load model rather than from EXPLAIN output. The two measurements I could make without a database (the cron's generated WHERE clause and drizzle's bind-parameter count for inArray) are quoted in the findings.
- (scalability) The Vercel plan and therefore the exact function duration limit is not in the repository (apps/web/vercel.json sets no maxDuration and only /api/nutrition/estimate exports one), so SCALE-1's cut-off point is bounded only as 'between 60 s and 800 s on current tiers' rather than named exactly.
- (scalability) packages/api/src/ai/policy.ts and the cron batching work exist only on the unmerged branch ai-policy-and-cron-batching, which is not present in this worktree, so I could not assess whether that branch already fixes SCALE-1 or SCALE-13.
- (scalability) Neon's pooled-endpoint client-connection ceiling and the compute size for this project are account settings, not repository facts, so SCALE-16's connection arithmetic (10 connections x instance count) cannot be compared to the actual ceiling.
- (scalability) apps/web/public/sw.js is generated by Serwist at build time and is gitignored; the inventory lists it as an entry point. I audited the source, apps/web/app/sw.ts, instead.
- (scalability) No concurrency, idempotency or load test exists anywhere: the baseline's 131 tests are all in packages/core (pure functions) plus one Playwright flow, and no test touches packages/api's services. None of the races in SCALE-3 to SCALE-6 is pinned by a test, so a fix cannot be verified by the existing suite and will need new tests (that gap itself belongs to dimension 9).
- (scalability) Graceful shutdown and liveness probes are not assessable as code findings on this host: Vercel manages instance lifecycle and there is no /api/health endpoint or SIGTERM handler to review. If the app is ever moved to the plain-VM Docker Compose target ARCHITECTURE.md gate 5 requires, both become real gaps - docker-compose.yml currently defines only postgres and redis, with no web service.
- (secrets) Whether `RESEND_API_KEY` is actually set on the production Vercel project. It decides whether SECRET-2 is leaking magic-link credentials and weekly letters into the function log today or is only a latent configuration trap; docs/DEPLOY.md:15 marks it optional, and the brief's known-broken item 2 shows at least one optional email variable is unset in production. I have no read access to the Vercel environment, and I did not probe the live sign-in form because that would mint tokens and create side effects on production. `vercel env ls production` settles it. If it is unset and the project's logs are readable by anyone beyond the owner, SECRET-2 is a P0 account-takeover path, not a P1.
- (secrets) Whether the `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET` and `HEALTH_DATA_ENCRYPTION_KEY` values in the owner's local .env (lines 6 and 40) are the same values as the ones on Vercel. docs/DEPLOY.md:11 says to generate fresh ones for production, and the local DATABASE_URL is localhost, so they are probably development-only - but if they were reused, a laptop compromise would decrypt production health data and forge production sessions. Only the owner can compare them against the Vercel values; neither value is in the repository or its history, so nothing needs rotating on account of the repo.
- (secrets) Whether the stray `@kettleworth.test` accounts named in the brief were created by the committed scripts and therefore carry the committed password. The two creation paths I can see (apps/web/live-multi.mjs:4, apps/web/e2e/core-flow.spec.ts:8) both use `correct-horse-battery`, but I cannot query the production database to confirm which accounts exist or what their password hashes are.
- (secrets) There is no application build image to audit. docker-compose.yml runs stock postgres:16-alpine and redis:7-alpine only, there is no Dockerfile anywhere in the repository, and deployment is `vercel --prod` (docs/DEPLOY.md:18), so the 'secrets baked into an image layer' class does not apply here.
- (secrets) Vercel's own log retention and access control cannot be assessed from the repository. apps/web/app/privacy/page.tsx:15 claims "Server logs are kept for 30 days", which I could not verify against any configured drain or retention setting; there is no log configuration in the repo.
- (security) Whether CRON_SECRET, MUX_WEBHOOK_SECRET and MODERATION_EMAIL are set in the Vercel project. I have no access to production environment variables, and the only probe that would settle CRON_SECRET (a GET to /api/cron/weekly) executes the all-users fan-out and emails real users if the secret is unset, so I deliberately did not run it. The working-tree .env, which matches .env.example key-for-key up to line 46, defines none of the three, and the brief confirms MODERATION_EMAIL is unset in production. SEC-1 and SEC-9 are written against the fail-open code, which is a defect whatever the current value is.
- (security) Whether the BETTER_AUTH_SECRET and HEALTH_DATA_ENCRYPTION_KEY in the working-tree .env are the same values configured on Vercel. I verified the question the brief actually asked, that neither matches the CI workflow's hardcoded values, but I cannot read Vercel's environment to confirm the local file is or is not a copy of production. Dimension 1 owns the secret inventory; note that .env also holds a live-shaped Google/YouTube API key (AIza..., 39 chars) and that .env is untracked and gitignored.
- (security) No test in the suite exercises a route handler, an authentication guard or an ownership check: all 21 files in the baseline are under packages/core and packages/integrations. Every authorisation conclusion in this report, including the mitigations, comes from reading the code path rather than from a test that sends the hostile request and asserts the refusal, and nothing in CI would catch a regression in any of them.
- (security) The production database state was not inspected. I have no production DATABASE_URL and would not connect to it, so I could not confirm how many stray @kettleworth.test accounts exist (SEC-13) or whether any report rows already block a delete (SEC-4).
- (security) Vercel's request body limit was not measured against the live deployment, so SEC-2's per-request ceiling may be the platform's ~4.5 MB rather than the 24 MB the code allows. The finding does not depend on it: there is no cap on the number of requests or on bytes per account.
- (security) The exact trigger configuration for GHSA-2464-fhr4-w9xw (Next.js image RCE) is not stated in the advisory, so SEC-7's RCE half is asserted only to the strength of the version check (next 16.3.5 in the affected range, sharp 0.35.4 below the stated workaround threshold); images.dangerouslyAllowSVG is not set, which may or may not be the relevant gate.
- (security) better-auth's password hashing parameters (scrypt cost factors) were not inspected against current guidance; I confirmed only that hashing is the library's and not a hand-rolled digest.
- (tests) Whether `pnpm test:e2e` passes locally today. Running it would sign up accounts and write training rows to a database, which is outside the read-only scope I was given, so I proved only the CI failure mode (TEST-2) with a throwaway spec in my scratch directory. Note that .env sets BETTER_AUTH_URL=http://localhost:3100 while the dev server and NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL are on 3000; I did not determine whether that affects the local sign-up step.
- (tests) Whether stray @kettleworth.test accounts are on the production database right now. I have no production DATABASE_URL and did not attempt a connection; I verified the mechanism that creates them (TEST-6) and that the purge script is manual, not the current row count.
- (tests) Coverage cannot be measured: no package depends on @vitest/coverage-v8 or any coverage reporter, and there is no coverage config, so I assessed by enumerating risky paths as 09-tests prescribes rather than by percentage.
- (tests) Whether `turbo run test`'s cache can mask a real break. I observed a fully cached run ('FULL TURBO', 30ms) and turbo's default inputs should invalidate on a source change, but I did not prove it by mutating a file, since I may not edit the project.
- (tests) UI and component behaviour (the three worlds, per-world compositions) is untested, but apps/web components are gated by the sensational-ui hook and a visual contract is reported rather than fixed in this run, so I did not assess what a component tier should pin there.
- (tests) Whether the GitHub Actions job is a required status check on main. `gh run list` shows 40 failures merged over, which implies it is not, but branch-protection settings were not readable with the access I used.

## Assumptions the run worked under

- (ai-safety) 'Customer-reachable' means reachable by any account that completes the free, unauthenticated sign-up the brief describes. On that reading one hostile stranger has all eight AI entry points, and that is the threat model used for severity throughout.
- (ai-safety) The audit is of the working tree at HEAD (branch `kw-weight`, commit 003fe0b, dirty), which like `main` contains no `packages/api/src/ai/policy.ts`. The fix for AI-1, AI-4 and AI-7 exists only on the unmerged branch `ai-policy-and-cron-batching` (commit 0bc0aaf); I verified the file list and commit message on that branch but audited the checked-out tree, as the brief directs.
- (ai-safety) ARCHITECTURE.md's design point (1M registered, 400k MAU, 200k DAU) and its price assumptions are taken as agreed and audited against rather than re-litigated, per the brief. Where a finding bites only at scale (AI-4, AI-7) the severity comes from the scale rules and the number is cited from ARCHITECTURE.md rather than re-derived.
- (ai-safety) The deterministic fallback is treated as a mitigation for availability, not for cost: a call that fails, is refused or is capped after the request has gone to the provider has still been billed, so 'it degrades gracefully' does not reduce the severity of a missing cap.
- (ai-safety) Prompt injection is assessed as self-scoped. No third-party-authored text reaches any prompt: the community service (`packages/api/src/services/community.ts`) makes no model call, and every prompt is assembled from the requesting account's own profile, logs and messages. An injection therefore affects only the injector's own reversible rows, which is why AI-10 is a P3 and not a P0.
- (ai-safety) An AI call made during a server page render (`apps/web/app/app/nutrition/page.tsx:22` calls `getOrBuildMealPlan`, which calls `mealPlanNote` when no plan exists for the week) is in scope but not written up as its own finding: it is week-idempotent, so the exposure is one burst of concurrent first-loads per account per week, and the per-call cost is covered by AI-1 and the render-latency consequence belongs to the performance dimension.
- (altitude) A .tsx file under apps/web/components or apps/web/app is treated as UI and therefore reported rather than fixed in place, per the brief's sensational-ui rule - even where the change is behavioural and touches no markup, colour, type or motion (LEAN-3's page-component half, LEAN-9). I have said so explicitly in each blast_radius so the fixer does not route a non-visual refactor through the design skill by accident, or around the hook.
- (altitude) The brief froze the stack, the schema, the migration chain, the URL space, the better-auth contract and the GDPR formats. Every fix proposed here is therefore an in-place extraction or a one-line predicate change; none adds a dependency, a table, a column or a route, and none is a rewrite. Where the honest answer would have been structural (`packages/api` has no tests at all), I recorded it as a mitigation gap rather than proposing a new test framework.
- (altitude) `BILLING_ENFORCED` and `CRON_SECRET` are unset in production, as the brief states for BILLING_ENFORCED and as the `if (secret && ...)` shape at cron/weekly/route.ts:9 implies is possible for CRON_SECRET. LEAN-5 and LEAN-7 are written to hold either way: LEAN-5 is dead code whatever the flag is, and LEAN-7's predicate is a tautology whatever the auth header is.
- (altitude) The three-strikes rule from the altitude reference is applied as written, so two copies are recorded but not raised as findings. The identical Escape-key-and-body-scroll-lock effect at apps/web/components/app/nav.tsx:59-63 and apps/web/components/marketing/site-header.tsx:38-42, and the shared dialog/sheet block at packages/ui/src/components/dialog.tsx:15 / sheet.tsx:24 that the inventory flagged, are two copies each and are deliberately left alone - extracting them would buy a shared hook for a UI file set that is gated anyway.
- (altitude) `checkPlausibility` returning 409 is intended product behaviour (an implausible set must be confirmed, session.ts:124-135), not a bug. LEAN-1 is written on that assumption: the defect is that the replay path discards the rejection instead of surfacing it, not that the rejection exists.
- (deployability) "Production" is the Vercel project serving kettleworth.vercel.app, built from the `main` branch of github.com/bbweareark/kettleworth, against Neon Postgres as docs/DEPLOY.md:3 states. The repository is public, which I treated as a fact about blast radius rather than as a finding (dimension 1's ground).
- (deployability) docs/DEPLOY.md and README.md are the project's own instructions, so I audited them as what a newcomer would follow literally, including the manual `vercel --prod` release at DEPLOY.md:21 - there is no deploy workflow in .github, so I took that laptop-driven path to be the real one.
- (deployability) Where platform limits mattered I used the project's own written numbers (DEPLOY.md:26, "serverless functions up to 10 s by default") rather than the vendor's current documentation, so the finding stands on the repository's own stated contract regardless of what Vercel's defaults are today.
- (deployability) Reading this repository's GitHub Actions history and branch-protection state through `gh` is in scope as read-only evidence about the pipeline, since the pipeline is the artefact under audit and its behaviour is not observable from the workflow file alone.
- (deployability) I treated the AI spend cap, the per-account rate limits and the unauthenticated reachability of /api/cron/weekly and /api/webhooks/mux as dimensions 3 and 2's findings. Where they appear here it is only as evidence of a configuration contract that is not validated (OPS-2) or a signal that is not collected (OPS-5), not as a second count of the same defect.
- (deployability) ARCHITECTURE.md is the agreed design point, so items it already records as deferred (no Dockerfile, ai_log retention, photo object storage, cron batching) were audited against its own plan rather than re-litigated. OPS-8 is written because the cron's failure is invisible today, not because batching is unfinished.
- (performance) ARCHITECTURE.md's load model is the design point to audit against, as the brief directs: 1M registered, 400k MAU, 200k DAU, ~555 RPS at peak with peak factor 3, exercise_instance ~500M rows/year, health_sample ~365M/year, ~250k async jobs/day.
- (performance) Where the brief gives no per-user behaviour I used: 3 Today renders per DAU per day, 20 logged sets per training day, 4 training days a week, and a 2-year account as the realistic upper end of history - each stated inline where it is used.
- (performance) Round-trip latency from a Vercel lhr1 function to Neon is taken as 5 ms for a co-located endpoint and 15 ms otherwise; the local measurements were taken at ~0.6 ms per statement and are scaled explicitly rather than quoted as production numbers.
- (performance) A Claude call at effort "medium" with max_tokens 1500 is taken as ~8 seconds end to end for the cron arithmetic in PERF-1; the conclusion does not depend on the figure, since even 1 second per user fails at 1M.
- (performance) The AI features are enabled in production (ANTHROPIC_API_KEY set), as the brief's AI-exposure section states; with the key unset every structured() call returns null and the cron's per-user cost collapses to the deterministic fallback, which changes PERF-1's timing but not its shape.
- (performance) COMMUNITY_DISCOVERY is off in production as .env.example sets it, so PERF-11's board and match reads are reachable through the partner/invite path and the Community page itself, but the stranger-discovery volume is not yet live.
- (performance) The local dev database at localhost:5434 is a safe, read-only target for EXPLAIN and SELECT; no statement run during this audit wrote to it, and no project file was created, edited or deleted outside the scratch directory.
- (quality) I audited the working tree as checked out (branch kw-weight at 003fe0b, dirty per .shipready/inventory.json), not the unmerged ai-policy-and-cron-batching branch. CODE-3's fan-out finding is about the cron as it stands here.
- (quality) The Vercel Node runtime runs with TZ=UTC, so a server-side `new Date().toISOString()` is the UTC calendar date. CODE-5 rests on that and on the kw-tz cookie being the only source of the user's zone (apps/web/lib/local-date.ts:9).
- (quality) `CRON_SECRET` is set in production, so /api/cron/weekly is not publicly callable; CODE-2 and CODE-3 are about what the scheduled run does, not about who can trigger it.
- (quality) Progress photos are stored in Postgres in production (`inDb()` is true when VERCEL is set and STORAGE_DIR is not, photos.ts:13), so the disk paths in savePhoto/readPhoto/deleteAllPhotos are dev-only. On that reading deleteAllPhotos' `rm` is a no-op in production and the hard delete relies on the `onDelete: cascade` at packages/db/src/schema/profile.ts:40, which is present - so there is no finding there.
- (quality) Visual and layout concerns are out of scope for this dimension and are not fixed here; where a defect lives in a client component it is reported as logic (CODE-1, CODE-5), not as a design change.
- (scalability) ARCHITECTURE.md's load model (1,000,000 registered, 400,000 MAU, 200,000 DAU, ~555 peak RPS, exercise_instance ~500M rows/year, ~250,000 async jobs/day) is the agreed design point and is audited against rather than re-litigated, as the brief instructs.
- (scalability) The deployed configuration is the one docs/DEPLOY.md describes: Vercel in lhr1, Neon Postgres over the pooled connection string, PHOTO_STORAGE=db, CRON_SECRET set, AI_MODEL=claude-opus-5.
- (scalability) 'More machines' means more Vercel instances against one Neon primary, which is what exists today: no read replica, no queue, no Redis consumer (docker-compose.yml declares redis but no code imports a client).
- (scalability) The database schema and the Drizzle migration chain are frozen for this run, so every fix proposed is code-side (transactions, row locks, conditional claims, pagination, batching, explicit projections). Where only an index or constraint would be the durable fix - audit_log(user_id, action, target) in SCALE-8, a unique (user_id, exercise_id, session_id) in SCALE-5, ai_log(user_id, created_at) in SCALE-13, and partitioning for exercise_instance and health_sample - I say so and mark it as an escalation rather than proposing it here.
- (scalability) Letters and adaptations are expected to reach every onboarded user every week (that is what the feature claims and what ARCHITECTURE.md:48 budgets 250,000 jobs/day for), so a cron that serves only its first page of users is a failure rather than an accepted partial rollout.
- (scalability) The AI spend cap, per-account request limits and model-tier-per-task are dimension 3's ground; I treat them as absent on main and only note where my dimension's fixes (the batch worker, shared rate-limit storage, an indexed spend query) are the mechanism those caps will need.
- (secrets) Vercel function logs count as a log the company controls, so a credential written there is P1 rather than the P0 reserved for 'a log the company does not control' - but only while log access is limited to the owner. If logs are shared with a team, a drain or an observability vendor, SECRET-2 escalates.
- (secrets) The brief freezes the Drizzle migration chain, so SECRET-1's fix is application-level (redact before logging, plus an explicit `delete(aiLog)` inside `deleteAccount`) rather than the foreign key with ON DELETE CASCADE that the schema should have had.
- (secrets) 'Live' for SECRET-3 means the committed password authenticates accounts that exist on the production database today, which I take from the brief's known-broken item 3 rather than from a query of my own.
- (secrets) The owner's local `.env` is a development file, on the evidence of its localhost DATABASE_URL, localhost BETTER_AUTH_URL and empty provider secrets; its live-shaped `YOUTUBE_API_KEY` (AIza..., 39 chars) is therefore treated as a local-machine key rather than a repository leak, since it is absent from history and from the build output. It is still a live Google API key on disk and worth rotating if the machine is ever shared.
- (security) Production is the Vercel deployment at kettleworth.vercel.app running with NODE_ENV=production, so useSecureCookies is true and better-auth's in-memory rate-limit store is per-lambda-instance rather than per-host.
- (security) The working-tree .env mirrors the production variable set. It is key-for-key .env.example up to line 46 and omits CRON_SECRET, MUX_WEBHOOK_SECRET, MODERATION_EMAIL, PHOTO_STORAGE and COMMUNITY_DISCOVERY, which is consistent with the brief's statement that MODERATION_EMAIL is unset in production.
- (security) PHOTO_STORAGE is unset and VERCEL is set in production, so packages/api/src/services/photos.ts:13 resolves inDb() to true and progress photographs are bytea rows in Neon, as the brief states.
- (security) A finding whose only consequence is Claude spend belongs to dimension 3. Where an input-validation or authorisation shape of mine feeds that (unbounded string lengths on apps/web/app/api/onboarding/followup/route.ts:6, the missing entitlement gate in SEC-11), I report the shape and leave the spend severity to them.
- (security) Sending a user's own health free text to Anthropic is the stated product function and has a lawful basis, so I did not treat it as an unlawful third-party transfer. The ai_log finding in SEC-4 is about the plaintext copy retained in the product's own database and surviving erasure, not about the transfer.
- (security) A read-only GET of the public sign-in page and the response headers of the live deployment is a safe, non-mutating check, and I used it to establish that no social provider is configured in production (decisive for SEC-6) and that no CSP is served (SEC-16). I made no authenticated, mutating or side-effectful request to production.
- (security) Group visibility is never set to private today because createGroup (packages/api/src/services/community.ts:153) does not pass the column, so SEC-10 is scored on the leave-and-still-read and non-member-comment cases rather than on private-group exposure.
- (tests) I audited the checked-out worktree at 003fe0b, which `git rev-parse` shows is identical to origin/main, so 'main' in the brief is what I read. The local `main` ref is stale (075c96c); I used origin/main.
- (tests) The documented suite command is `pnpm run test` (root package.json:13 -> `turbo run test`), as the baseline records, and `pnpm test:e2e` is a separate manual tier that the documented command does not include.
- (tests) GitHub Actions (.github/workflows/ci.yml, the only workflow file) is the only automated test gate; Vercel builds and deploys independently of it, which I did not verify in the Vercel project settings.
- (tests) A test that cannot be run by the documented command is treated as absent, which is why packages/db/packages/types/apps/web count as uncovered rather than merely untested.
- (tests) I treated the brief's four known-broken items as given and only assessed them where they bear on tests (the stray .test accounts in TEST-6, the AI cap harness in TEST-7); I did not re-derive them.
- (tests) Removing one artifact my own probe created: the Playwright run wrote /Users/basilbaby/Projects/kettleworth-main/test-results/probe-goto-relative (a gitignored path that did not exist before), and I deleted it to leave the tree as I found it. The probe itself is under my scratch directory.

## Proposed fix waves (written for the owner, 27 Nov 2026)

The generated job grouping put almost every file in one job, which is not workable: two
agents cannot own one file, and several fixes are product decisions rather than repairs.
What follows is the order I propose instead. Nothing has been edited.

### Wave 0 - the live exposure, today (no product decision, no contract change)

`pnpm audit --prod` fails right now with **4 critical advisories** against the deployed app:

| Package | Installed | Advisory | Patched |
|---|---|---|---|
| better-auth | <1.7.7 | **OAuth state can be used as a magic link to sign in as another user** - account takeover | >=1.7.7 |
| next | 16.3.5 | **Unauthenticated remote code execution in Image Optimization**, plus RCE in `use cache` and in `next/og` | >=16.4.1 |
| sharp | <0.35.5 | RCE via librsvg (CVE-2026-96889) | >=0.35.5 |
| zod | <4.6.6 | memory exhaustion on oversized collections | >=4.6.6 |

All four are patch or minor bumps inside the declared ranges, so only the lockfile moves.
This is the cheapest and highest-value change in the whole record, and it is the only one
that is being exploited-shaped today on a public site.

### Wave 1 - the P0s that are bounds, not decisions

| Finding | Fix | Touches |
|---|---|---|
| AI-2 | `.max()` on the three free-text fields the followup route interpolates, plus a cap inside `structured()` | 1 route, 1 client |
| LEAN-NEW5 / AI-6 / AI-NEW2 | stop forwarding the service's internal opts bag; `email` becomes server-side only, `force` costs a week | 2 routes, 2 services |
| SEC-2 | per-account photo quota (count and bytes) in `savePhoto`, not only at the handler | 1 service, 1 route |
| CODE-NEW1 | the weekly cron's bearer check is fail-open when `CRON_SECRET` is unset - close it | 1 route |

### Wave 2 - the AI spend cap and the cron fan-out (needs one decision from the owner)

`packages/api/src/ai/policy.ts` on branch `ai-policy-and-cron-batching` (commit `0bc0aaf`)
already implements the per-account cap with 12 unit tests, and the cursor-batched cron.
Merging it closes **AI-1, AI-4, PERF-1, SCALE-1 and SCALE-2** at once.

**The decision:** batching the cron also changes *who gets a weekly letter* - today every
profile row does, including accounts that never finished intake. That is a product change,
so it is the owner's call, not a repair.

### Wave 3 - the long tail

94 further confirmed P1/P2/P3: the N+1s and unbounded scans on the Today page, the missing
indexes, the offline-queue double-replay, `logSet`'s lost-update race, the server test tier
that does not exist, observability, and the runbook. These are real and they are not
emergencies at today's load.

### Escalated, not fixed here (contract changes)

- progress photos out of Postgres into object storage (`ARCHITECTURE.md` already plans it)
- `ai_log` / `audit_log` into the delete cascade and the GDPR export - a schema change that
  touches the two contracts this run was told not to change
- the two `ON DELETE NO ACTION` foreign keys that make GDPR hard delete throw

## What the independent verifier found, 27 Nov 2026

The seven P0s were fixed and then handed to a separate agent that had not seen the work,
with the diff and instructions to be hard on it. It was, and it was right. Its rulings:

| Finding | Ruling | What I did about it |
|---|---|---|
| SEC-2 photo quota | **PASS** | — |
| CODE-NEW1 cron fail-open | **PASS** | — |
| SEC-7 / four criticals | **PASS** | — |
| LEAN-NEW5 | `email` **PASS**, `force` **REGRESSION** | Fixed. The meal plan's Rebuild button still posted `force`, got the cached plan back and toasted "New week planned" - a button that reports work it did not do. It is now Refresh, and the copy says what happened. |
| AI-2 input caps | **WEAK** | Fixed. I capped the three fields the finding named and left a bigger channel into the same prompt: `intakeTranscript` (unbounded array of unbounded strings, written by `PATCH /api/profile`) and four unbounded `z.array(z.string())` on the profile, both interpolated by `ai/tasks.ts`. All six are bounded now. |
| AI-1 spend cap | **WEAK** | Partly. See below. |
| AI-4 / PERF-1 / SCALE-1 / SCALE-2 cron | **WEAK** | Partly. See below. |

It also caught two dead functions of mine - `capUserText` and `mayRegenerate` - shipped with
six green tests, one set of which asserted a once-a-week rule **the product does not have**.
That is precisely the theatre this skill exists to stop, in the fix rather than the code
under review. Both are deleted along with the tests that described them.

### Still open after the verification, and honestly so

- **The AI cap fails open on the error it will actually get.** `monthlyUsage` aggregates
  `ai_log` filtered by `(user_id, created_at)`; the only index on that table is
  `(task, created_at)`. The lookup is wrapped in a try/catch that leaves the cap unapplied,
  so the first statement timeout at scale removes the ceiling. The index is a migration and
  is escalated, not smuggled in. **This is the single most important open item.**
- **The cap is check-then-act.** Usage is counted from rows written after the call returns,
  so N concurrent requests all read the same number. The per-account rate limiter (SEC-5)
  is what closes that, and it is not done.
- **The cron fan-out is still serial.** Batching stops one invocation having to finish a
  million users, but 200 awaited AI calls still will not fit in one. `maxDuration` is now
  set to the platform maximum, which buys room rather than solving it; the real answer is a
  queue, and it is open.
- **`generatedBy` on `coach_letter` records `AI_MODEL`** (the premium constant) even when
  the letter ran on the standard tier. `ai_log` has the true model, so the bill is right and
  the per-row provenance is wrong.
- Six high advisories remain, all build-time transitives on no request path.
